Investing - Theory, News &amp; General • Re: When the market tanks, what do you plan to do in retirement?

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

I retired 20 years ago. Now 83 and wife 81:
Current AA is around 61% equities, 32% fixed income and rest in cash or silver/gold ETF (I violated most Bogelheads thoughts on commodities)

In the last few years. I’ve gone from less ETF index equities to more ETF short term bonds and ETF munis. Depending on mental and physical health I will probably continue to go less with equities and more with bonds in AA. I’m finished with adding any more money to gold and silver.

With RMD requirements I may still take a little extra out of the traditional IRA to gift to children. Plus, we will use some of the IRA for QCD.

We have small pension so we live off of our RMD, SS, and dividends. We’re hoping we never have to sell our equity assets for our adult children to inherit.
Did you retire right before the 2008 financial crisis, then? How did you navigate those years as a new retiree?
My old boss at work retired and cashed out his pension lump sum in 2007. He was in dire straits in 2008. I remember he told his wife she could only have a tennis club membership if the DOW ever got back to 11,000. It seemed like a longshot at the time. He is pretty happy now with the Dow nearly five times that. And she loves her tennis club.

Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: No Surprises Act and emergent care

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If you get really sick or a bad car crash or whatever and need a transfer via helicopter you're probably looking at $100k for the flight alone, plus all other relevant charges so how lucky do you feel?

I never thought it'd happen to me either, but a few years ago my pregnant wife and unborn son got the $100k helicopter ride. Plus an ER visit, a ground ambulance transfer, stays at two ICUs, and all the various lab/radiology and professional fees to go along with. We could have handled it without insurance coverage, but we would have been just about broke after.

Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: Catastrophic Health Insurance 2027

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

When I last investigated this a few years back the differences in coverage and premium between a catastrophic plan and the nearest similar bronze plan were negligible. Apparently however this can vary by state and whatever carriers are servicing a given area.
This is going to be extremely YMMV based on where you live. Last year when I investigated, it was indeed the case that there was little benefit to taking a catastrophic plan.

This year things changed, and I would likely save a lot on a catastrophic plan. Irony is I no longer qualify (See above posts). But for reference, my options this year include:

Florida Blue MyBlue Catastrophic, $942/mo premium, $12K deductible, $12K max OOP (which I don't qualify for)
Florida Blue MyBlue Bronze, $1178/mo premium, $11975 deductible, $12K max OOP (yes this is virtually the same as the Catastrophic plan)
Amerihealth Bronze Enhanced, $1383/mo premium, $4500 deductible, $12K max OOP

So right now I'm deciding between what's effectively a catastrophic plan, or a plan with a more reasonable deductible that's going to cost $2460 more per year.
What really needs to happen IMO is very high deductible plans need to be added to the exchange, say maybe two options of $25,000 or $50,000 deductible with no annual coverage limits, and very low premiums of $100-$300 depending on age ranging from youngest to age 64. This way at people have SOME kind of option to protect their assets from being wiped out due to a major unforeseen health event while not robbing them blind every month for a plan most otherwise could be simply paying out of pocket to cover monthly medical expenses (if any).
Agree 100%. I don't understand why I can't buy this product; it's exactly what I want/need. And before anyone answers "The reason is that it would not be profitable", my response is "How do we know?" Insurers aren't allowed to offer a plan like this due to ACA requirements.
I've been watching for this over the last few years and have yet to see anything impeding the ability to offer such plans other than the carriers, hospitals and lobbyists. At this point I am fully convinced the only reason carriers aren't offering it is because some people would shift from other plans and reduce their existing profits, and hospitals may be stuck trying to collect on bills larger than they "prefer" to chase if people end up not paying. Many doctors & hospitals would actually rather deny service up front rather than have to accept insurance with a big deductible they may have to chase payment for.

What is your current marginal tax rate for Federal and State Income tax?
30% or so the last I recall.
It means that if you contribute $1,000 to a traditional 401k and your marginal tax rate is 30%, you save about $300 in taxes right away. If your marginal tax rate is lower in retirement, which from the numbers you provided looks likely, then contributing to the 401k makes even more sense.

These Are the Bestselling Electric Pickup Trucks

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A total of 232,809 electric pickup trucks have sold since the start of 2024. Ford alone sells nearly that many gas-powered F-series pickups every single quarter.

Electric pickup trucks are still a relatively new idea, but they've now been along long enough to gather some data on how they're selling. We totaled sales numbers from 2024, 2025, and 2026 so far to see which electric pickup trucks have had the biggest impact on the market in the past few years.

For some perspective, electric pickups still make up a tiny portion of the overall truck market in the U.S. Even if you combine the sales of all six models listed here, over a time period of nearly three years, you get 232,809 units sold in total. Ford sold 801,525 units of the F-series pickup in 2025 alone. There are also aren’t any pickup trucks on our list of the bestselling EVs in 2026, a list which contains mostly SUVs.

The sales totals for each model listed here include figures from the full-year 2024, the full-year 2025, and through the third quarter of 2026. In cases where the automaker doesn't release detailed sales figures, we used estimates from Cox Automotive.

If you feel like you see Cybertrucks everywhere you go, that's not just because their distinctive styling makes them stand out on the road. Tesla has sold quite a few pickup trucks in the past few years, which may seem surprising given the Cybertruck's polarizing design and numerous recalls early in its production run. Tesla started selling the Cybertruck in 2024, and that was the strongest year of sales, suggesting that eager early adopters made up much of the early run of 38,965 units sold that year. Sales declined to around 20,000 units in 2025, and through the third quarter of 2026 Tesla has moved an estimated 11,586 units.

Ford was one of the first to enter the electric-truck market, and its strategy hinged on the F-150 Lightning's similarities to the top-selling F-150 pickup. The F-150 Lightning has since been discontinued, ending production in 2025, but it managed to rack up a significant number of sales. Keep in mind that the Lightning went on sale for the 2022 model year, well before many of the other models on this list, so the sales totals here reflect only the latter half of its production run.

General Motors' Hummer EV pickup was also early to the trend, debuting for the 2022 model year like the F-150 Lightning. But it took a completely different approach with its wild styling, mega-horsepower powertrain, and ridiculous off-road capability. GMC added an SUV sibling for 2024, and the caveat to the Hummer's sales numbers are that they include both body styles. We aren't able to break out numbers for just the pickup, because GM doesn't report those figures separately.

Chevy's electric version of the Silverado pickup got off to a slow start, racking up less than 8000 sales in 2024, its first year on the market. Those numbers picked up for 2025 as Chevrolet added lower-cost variants meant for fleets, cresting 11,000 units that year. But it appears demand has softened once again, as Chevy only moved 5327 units through the first three quarters of this year.

Rivian's debut model was the R1T, which was exactly as innovative as you would expect from a startup automaker. With impressive performance, futuristic styling, and new ideas such as a storage tunnel between the cab and the bed, the R1T aimed more at lifestyle buyers than at traditional pickup customers. The R1T went on sale for the 2022 model year, and sales have been on a steady decline since 2024, Rivian's recently launched compact R2 SUV is now outselling the larger R1 lineup, which makes us wonder if there is room for Rivian to add a pickup version of that model that would offer a lower cost of entry.

Riding on the same platform as the Chevrolet Silverado EV, the Sierra EV aims for a middle ground between GM's other electric pickups. It's more upscale than its Chevy sibling, especially in Denali trim, but far more conventional-looking than the other GMC EV truck, the wild Hummer EV. Its sales numbers followed a similar trend as the Chevy, with a slow start in 2024, a rise in 2025, and a drop again in 2026.

Despite being raised on a steady diet of base-model Hondas and Toyotas—or perhaps because of it—Joey Capparella cultivated an obsession for the automotive industry throughout his childhood in Nashville, Tennessee. He found a way to write about cars for the school newspaper during his college years at Rice University, which eventually led him to move to Ann Arbor, Michigan, for his first professional auto-writing gig at Automobile Magazine. He has been part of the Hearst Autos team since 2016 and now lives in New York City.

Changes to Chile’s Human Rights Institute Risk Leaving Victims Behind

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Click to expand Image Chile's President Jose Antonio Kast waves on his inauguration day at Congress in Valparaiso, Chile, March 11, 2026. © 2026 Esteban Felix/AP Photo

Chilean President José Antonio Kast introduced in Congress on October 19 a proposal to change the country’s National Human Rights Institute.

Founded in 2009 as an autonomous state body, the institute has worked to promote and protect human rights in Chile, including by taking legal action on behalf of human rights abuse victims.

If passed, the legislation would weaken some of the tools the institute uses to protect human rights and diminish its independence.

The bill would remove the institute’s eleven-member board of directors, replacing them with a new five-member transitional board that would draft new bylaws for the institute. Unlike the current board, which has four members who were selected directly by civil society groups, the new transitional board would be proposed by the president, approved by the Senate, and would have no members selected directly by civil society. This sits uneasily with the standards set out in the Principles relating to the Status of National Human Rights Institutions (the Paris Principles), which recommend that members be independent from the government and selected through a process that ensures broad civil society representation.

According to a financial report by the Finance Ministry’s Budget Office, the proposal would also reduce the institute’s staff. The number of staff members in the institute’s regional offices outside of Santiago de Chile would be cut in half, and the number of lawyers in the institute’s protection unit, who handle the institute’s legal actions, would be reduced by 70 percent. As a result, people in many parts of Chile could lose access to in-person assistance from the institute.

The proposed legislation would also end the institute’s ability to represent victims in criminal cases—a power it has often used in cases of torture, ill treatment, or human trafficking. That would mean that victims who cannot afford a lawyer would lose legal support and would rely on prosecutors to decide whether to pursue their case and how to move it forward.

The National Human Rights Institute itself has welcomed other parts of the bill meant to improve its transparency and governance but has warned that some aspects of the bill would undermine its work. Chile’s Congress should reexamine these worrying provisions and make sure the institute can protect the rights of all Chileans.

8 foods that may help you sleep, from tart cherries to chamomile tea

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Tart cherries, night milk, kiwi, and chamomile tea appear among 8 foods the Sleep Foundation links to better rest.

Anyone who has gulped a second cup of coffee to survive a long afternoon, or dozed on the couch after Thanksgiving dinner, has felt food tilt a person toward alertness or drowsiness. The same plate that props up a workday can shape the hours after dark. Diet and sleep are both intricate systems, and each one reaches into the other. No single snack guarantees a quiet night, and nothing on a grocery shelf works as a magic switch. Certain foods and drinks may make a full night of rest easier to reach. Some contain nutrients and compounds that influence sleep-related hormones and brain activity, from melatonin production to blood sugar levels that hold steady overnight.

The body's sleep chemistry depends on what arrives on the plate. Melatonin, a hormone the pineal gland produces, signals when sleep should begin and keeps the circadian rhythm on schedule, and its output tends to fall with age. Tryptophan, an essential amino acid, must come from food because the body cannot make it. The body converts it into melatonin and into serotonin, a neurotransmitter tied to sleep, mood, and pain. Carbohydrates help the brain process tryptophan. The wrong late-night choices can undercut these processes. Citrus, tomatoes, and other acidic foods can raise stomach acid and bring on reflux. One study found that caffeine a person drank six hours before bed still affected sleep quality, and the stimulant has an average half-life of five hours in most healthy adults. Whole foods may hold advantages over pills. They deliver vitamins, minerals, and micronutrients that work in concert, along with fiber, which tracks with deeper sleep, and antioxidants that help ward off disease. Anyone weighing a supplement to fill a nutritional gap should consult a health care provider before starting it.

Sleep Foundation built its guide to sleep-friendly foods and drinks on 24 references, most of them published studies, and reports no original testing of its own. Eric Suni wrote the piece, which bears an Aug. 5, 2025, update date. Lulu Guo, MD, a board-certified sleep medicine physician, reviewed it for medical accuracy. Guo urges readers to focus on healthy eating patterns across the whole day instead of pinning their hopes on one food or drink. The guide sorts its picks by the nutrient they supply, with sections for melatonin, tryptophan, and magnesium and a fourth set of foods that work through other compounds. It lists foods to skip before bed and answers common questions about supplements and caffeine timing. Advertising supports the site, which says it does not endorse third-party products. The eight foods that follow form a selection from that longer guide, in the order the source presents them, and they appear without any ranking.

Tart cherries hold above-average concentrations of melatonin, the hormone that cues sleep, and the Sleep Foundation names that trait as one possible reason the fruit may help people doze off. Some shoppers know them as sour cherries, a label that captures how their flavor departs from the sweet varieties. The group spans several cultivars, among them Richmond, Montmorency, and English morello.

Grocers stock the fruit whole or pressed into tart cherry juice. Bakers reach for the tart type in pies and other baked goods, and fresh tart cherries can be tough to find, according to Cleveland Clinic. Scarce fresh fruit explains why most recommendations steer people toward the juice over the whole cherry. A bottle gives the easiest entry point for anyone hoping to test the fruit at bedtime.

Several studies have found that people who drink tart cherry juice sleep better. Melatonin may not account for the whole benefit. The Sleep Foundation adds that the cherries may exert an antioxidant action that favors sleep, a second possible route to a better night.

A Cleveland Clinic expert noted that tart cherries also contain tryptophan. The expert called it plausible that the amino acid and the fruit's melatonin could help people relax and sleep better. The body converts tryptophan into melatonin, which means each serving may deliver the hormone itself along with a building block for more.

Cleveland Clinic reports that early research on the juice has been small and short on rigor. The small studies suggest the drink may lengthen sleep and improve its quality, and scientists need more work to confirm the signal. The Cleveland Clinic expert cautioned that strong scientific evidence for a sleep benefit is still lacking. Two candidate mechanisms and a string of encouraging findings make tart cherries a credible bedtime option, one still built on early evidence.

Milk drawn at night holds nearly 10 times more melatonin than milk collected during the day. The evening yield goes by the name night milk, and the Sleep Foundation identifies milking time as a major influence on how much of the sleep hormone the drink contains. Flip the comparison and a daytime glass carries about one-tenth of the melatonin in an equal pour of night milk.

The gap turns milk into a drink whose sleep chemistry shifts with the clock. Two glasses can look identical yet differ several times over in the hormone that cues the body to wind down. Among the foods the Sleep Foundation credits with melatonin, milk stands out because the hour of collection helps set the dose.

Night milk produced calming, sedative, and sleep-promoting effects in mice, and researchers likened its impact to diazepam. The result comes from animal research and cannot predict how people will respond. Even so, the mouse findings suggest the extra hormone in evening milk can do measurable work in a living body.

Warm milk at bedtime has a long history as a home remedy, and the Sleep Foundation argues that real science supports the drink as a sleep aid. A heated mug may also help through comfort. If a warm cup relaxes someone as part of a nightly routine, it could ease the way to sleep even when the benefit amounts to a placebo.

Milk offers two separate paths to rest. One runs through the melatonin it carries, a supply concentrated in milk drawn after dark. The other runs through the soothing habit of a warm drink before bed, which can work whatever the hormone count. A sleeper chasing the chemical benefit would gain the most from night milk, while the ritual benefit asks for nothing more than a warm mug and a bedtime routine.

Fatty fish supplies vitamin D and omega-3 fatty acids, two nutrients researchers believe can help people sleep. Each one has a hand in how the body releases serotonin and keeps its levels in balance.

Fish also turns up on the Sleep Foundation's list of tryptophan sources, next to turkey, chicken, eggs, cheese, edamame, tofu, peanuts, quinoa, and pumpkin seeds. The amino acid serves as raw material for serotonin, so a single serving touches two stages of the same chain. Tryptophan builds the neurotransmitter, and vitamin D and omega-3s help govern its release.

Pairing fish with carbohydrates may give its tryptophan a clearer path. The guide says a moderate portion of tryptophan-bearing protein eaten alongside carbs may help the amino acid reach the brain. It traces the drowsiness people blame on turkey to that same pairing. A fish fillet over rice fits the pattern the source sets out for poultry.

The guide presents the fish benefit as a belief researchers hold and attaches no serving size or expected effect to it. Fatty fish earns its place through nutrients that act on serotonin from both ends, though the guide leaves the size of any sleep payoff unmeasured.

Cheese delivers a high dose of tryptophan, and the Sleep Foundation credits that amino acid with most of the sleep benefit linked to dairy. Some research has found a strong connection between dairy foods such as cheese and the quality of a person's rest, the guide reports.

Cheese appears on the guide's quick-reference list of tryptophan sources, alongside turkey, chicken, fish, eggs, edamame, tofu, peanuts, quinoa, and pumpkin seeds. It stands as the lone milk-based entry among those 10 foods. The listing gives people who skip meat and fish one more option, beside eggs and plant foods such as tofu, for an evening meal or snack.

Tryptophan gives cheese a sleep pathway separate from milk's, whose appeal in the guide hinges on the melatonin it holds. The guide's entry on cheese names no melatonin content and leans mainly on the amino acid, which hands the body raw material for building its own sleep chemistry. The entry also points to serotonin, calcium, and selenium, a mineral whose antioxidant properties may improve sleep. Cheese thus arrives with one finished sleep compound, serotonin, alongside the building block, plus a mineral that works on a separate front.

Calcium turns up in milk and yogurt as well as cheese, and the body draws on the mineral to turn tryptophan into melatonin. AARP reports that nutritionist Bonnie Taub-Dix called cheese a protein-rich snack and said its calcium, magnesium, and tryptophan are all needed for good sleep. Her list adds magnesium to the nutrients a wedge of cheese brings to the plate.

Carbohydrates may ease the trip cheese's tryptophan makes into the brain, according to the guide, which says a moderate serving of tryptophan-bearing protein eaten with carbs may smooth that route. Its turkey section explains that carbohydrates help the amino acid cross the blood-brain barrier and lift serotonin levels. One bedtime snack idea tops whole-grain crackers with cheese. Such a pairing places protein and carbohydrate in the same bite, the mix the guide describes. Cheese eaten alone may do less for sleep than cheese eaten with a starch.

One cup of firm tofu holds 593 milligrams of tryptophan, which equals 212% of the recommended daily intake. A single cup of the firm block more than doubles a full day's target for the amino acid.

Soy foods appear twice on the Sleep Foundation's quick list of tryptophan sources, as edamame and as tofu. The list also names turkey, chicken, fish, eggs, cheese, peanuts, quinoa, and pumpkin seeds. Plants supply half of its 10 entries. Diners who skip meat or dairy can reach the amino acid through soy, which the guide describes as a group of foods with solid amounts of it.

Tofu's tryptophan may go further with a starchy partner. Elsewhere in the guide, the Sleep Foundation notes that a moderate amount of tryptophan-bearing protein eaten alongside carbohydrates may help the amino acid reach the brain. Its turkey entry makes the same point from another angle, crediting carbohydrates with helping tryptophan cross the blood-brain barrier and lift serotonin levels. Tofu served with rice, another food the guide covers, fits that pattern.

Soybeans carry isoflavones, a class of phytoestrogen. In the guide's account, these plant compounds may mimic human estrogen, a hormone that helps govern how long and how well people sleep. The source treats this hormonal link as a possibility and stops short of calling it proven. Its wording puts the isoflavone claim on softer footing than the tofu figure, which arrives as a measured quantity.

Soy may act on rest through two separate routes, supplying tryptophan in quantity while its isoflavones work on a hormonal pathway of their own. Among the eight foods in this selection, it stands alone in reaching sleep through an estrogen-like compound.

One cup of uncooked oats packs 66% of the daily value for magnesium and 130% of the recommended daily intake for tryptophan. The tryptophan share overshoots a full day's target by 30 percentage points, which means a single bowl meets the day's entire need for the amino acid and leaves a surplus. The magnesium share falls a fraction short of two-thirds of a day's requirement in the same serving. Oats hold close to double the magnesium and five times the tryptophan of a cup of boiled spinach, which the guide credits with 37% of the daily value for magnesium and 26% of the recommended intake for tryptophan.

Complex carbohydrates give oats a second sleep mechanism. The Sleep Foundation reports that these carbohydrates lift serotonin and reduce cortisol, a stress hormone capable of interfering with sleep. Turkey, by the guide's account, needs carbohydrates beside it on the plate to produce its familiar drowsiness. Oats bring the amino acid and the carbohydrates to the table in one food, with no side dish necessary. The guide tempers the carbohydrate case with a caveat, noting that research on carbohydrates and sleep has produced mixed results.

Old-fashioned, quick-cooking, and instant oats all belong to the rolled oat family, and thinner rolling shortens the time on the stove. Old-fashioned oats take the longest of the three and reward the wait with a heartier, chewier texture. Steel cut oats, also known as Irish oats, skip steaming and rolling and require more simmering than any other variety. Water and milk both work as the base liquid, while stock or broth turns the pot savory.

Overnight oats call for little or no heat. Old-fashioned or quick-cooking oats soak through the night in low-fat milk, then take a topping of fruit.

Oats supply plenty of fiber and several B vitamins, a profile they hold in common with barley, farro, and brown rice. The Sleep Foundation counts fiber among the whole-food advantages tied to deeper, more restorative sleep. Measured against the guide's own nutrient framework, oats reach three of its four sleep pathways: tryptophan, magnesium, and complex carbohydrates.

Two kiwis eaten an hour before bedtime helped people drift off sooner, stay asleep longer, and give their rest higher marks, according to the Sleep Foundation. The gains reach three separate stretches of the night. Falling asleep, staying asleep, and the perceived quality of slumber all improved in the same finding. One small snack touched the start, the length, and the feel of a night in bed.

Kiwifruit packs numerous vitamins and minerals. The Sleep Foundation singles out four standouts: vitamins C and E, potassium, and folate. A fruit eaten before lights-out brings that payload along with whatever influence it has on sleep.

Nobody has pinned down why kiwi helps. Researchers suspect antioxidant properties that suppress inflammatory markers. The fruit may correct folate deficiencies or act through serotonin, and some mix of these could account for the effect.

Folate links two threads of the evidence. The nutrient appears among kiwi's featured contents, and one candidate explanation credits the fruit with making up for a shortage of that same nutrient. People low in folate could gain the most if that theory holds.

Serotonin connects kiwi to a pathway the guide traces through several other foods. Cheese, tofu, and oats supply tryptophan, the raw material the body uses to build that neurotransmitter. Kiwi carries a high concentration of serotonin itself, and scientists count the trait among the possible reasons for its benefit. If the idea proves out, the fruit would arrive with the finished compound in hand, while those foods offer the building block.

Among the eight foods in this selection, kiwi alone comes with both a set portion and a set time to eat it. Tofu and oats list serving sizes, yet neither includes advice on when to eat. With the cause unsettled, the timing becomes the most usable part of the result. Anyone can copy a pair of fruit an hour before bed without waiting for the science to catch up.

Chamomile tea owes its drowsy reputation to apigenin, a flavonoid the Sleep Foundation identifies as the compound thought to bring on the effect. The plant chemical has anxiolytic and sedative properties, which allow it to act on the central nervous system. A cup may ease anxiety and help the body unwind.

Apigenin sets the drink apart from most other foods in this selection. Tart cherries, milk, tofu, and oats earn attention for melatonin, tryptophan, or magnesium, nutrients tied to the hormones and brain chemistry that govern sleep. Chamomile follows a separate path. Its flavonoid targets the restless, keyed-up state that can hold off slumber.

The Sleep Foundation frames apigenin's role as a working theory, and research in people lends that theory some weight. AARP reports that a 2017 study tested chamomile extract on adults age 60 and older. The group taking the extract posted significant gains in sleep quality over a placebo group. Hunnes, an expert in the same AARP feature, also credits apigenin with encouraging sleepiness, which puts the outlet's account in line with the Sleep Foundation's.

The study measured an extract, not a brewed cup, so the dose a mug of tea delivers remains an open question. Its participants also came from a single age group. Chamomile's clearest promise lies in calming an anxious mind before bed, and the firmest human evidence for it so far comes from older adults.

Pennsylvania is way ahead on NEVI charging – now comes another $37M

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Pennsylvania is putting $37 million in federal NEVI funding toward what PennDOT says will be 515 new public EV charging ports across the Philadelphia region, including curbside chargers.

The awards cover Bucks, Chester, Delaware, Montgomery, and Philadelphia counties. PennDOT says the projects will expand public charging availability by 27% across the region and 53% in Philadelphia.

For drivers without a driveway, curbside charging gives them somewhere to plug in while parked near home.

It’s Electric PA received $2.93 million for 157 on-street Level 2 ports across 13 Philadelphia neighborhoods. Voltpost received funding for another 62 curbside ports across the four suburban counties.

Fast charging is getting funding, too. PositivEnergy received $7.2 million for 54 DC fast-charging ports at 11 Philadelphia Parking Authority lots. Tesla received two $392,000 awards for eight ports each at Royal Farms in Broomall and Wawa in Plymouth Meeting.

The money comes from the federal National Electric Vehicle Infrastructure (NEVI) program, created by the Biden administration’s Bipartisan Infrastructure Law. Pennsylvania’s five-year allocation is $171.5 million.

NEVI initially focused on fast charging along designated highway corridors. States can spend the money more broadly after the Federal Highway Administration certifies that their corridor charging requirements have been met. Pennsylvania received that certification on September 22, 2025.

In fact, Pennsylvania has built 54 EV charging stations using NEVI funds so far – more than double the number of any other state.

PennDOT’s community charging program supports both Level 2 and DC fast charging. Its published program minimum requirements include at least four ports per proposed station, at least 6 kW per port, two ADA-compliant parking spaces, and J1772 or CCS connectors on every port. Equipment must also meet Buy America requirements.

PennDOT lists 71 awards in this southeastern round as part of its roughly $100 million statewide community charging effort.

Applications for the western region close November 20. Central and eastern Pennsylvania applications close March 12, 2027.

For the southeastern projects, PennDOT’s estimated schedule puts environmental reviews in November 2026 through January 2027, followed by agreement execution in January and February. Drivers will still have to wait for construction and commissioning before these awards become places to plug in.

Former High Museum COO Sentenced to One Year in Prison for Siphoning $600,000 to Buy Guitars

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A federal judge sentenced the former chief operating officer of the High Museum of Art in Atlanta to one year in prison and one year of supervised release for stealing more than $600,000 from the museum.

Brady Lum, 60, served as the High Museum’s COO from January 2019 until December 2025, when he resigned. He began siphoning money from the museum in September 2019, around nine months into the job, until November 2025, when it was discovered.

Lum’s theft involved the purchase of “non-business items and services for himself, including expensive guitars and other music equipment, personal music lessons, and woodworking equipment, through direct supplier invoicing and the High Museum’s corporate credit card reimbursement process,” according to a press release from the Office of US Attorney for the Northern District of Georgia, which prosecuted the case. Related Articles High Museum of Art's Former COO Pleads Guilty to Embezzling More Than $600,000 $50,000 Driskell Prize Goes to Cheryl Finley of Spelman College

The release adds that Lum concealed the theft by “submitting altered invoices, using his position to approve expenses, and using accounting adjustments to spread his expenses across different cost centers.”

“Lum brazenly pilfered hundreds of thousands of dollars from a beloved civic institution to pay for his high-end guitars and other personal items. His appalling breach of trust damaged not only the High Museum but also the larger art community,” US Attorney Theodore S. Hertzberg said in a statement.

Prior to the sentencing on Monday, Lum had paid in full the $607,688.18 he had taken as part of his plea deal, which was announced in July. He also voluntarily paid the High Museum $500,000 to cover its investigative expenses and legal fees.

“Lum repaid the High Museum by covering its losses and expenses, and now he will pay his debt to society from prison,” Hertzberg added.

A request for comment from the High Museum was not immediately returned.

Magic: The Gathering Is Reprinting a Powerful $30 Sword With Perfect Pixel Art

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Magic: The Gathering is reaching into its inventory of powerful artifacts to reprint a sword worth nearly $30, complete with gloriously nostalgic video game artwork. Sword of Truth and Justice, a popular Equipment first released in 2019, is returning in a new Secret Lair collection that draws inspiration from the blocky, polygonal graphics of the Nintendo 64 and original PlayStation era.

Announced by Wizards of the Coast on November 3, the Rise of the Polygons | Extra Life 2026 drop features four reprinted cards and an Elemental token, with half of the purchase price benefiting children's hospitals. The $39.99 non-foil collection also includes another valuable reprint in Balefire Dragon, making this a potentially enticing proposition for Commander players, collectors, and anyone who still gets misty-eyed thinking about Ocarina of Time (or its remake).

Originally released in 2019's Modern Horizons, Sword of Truth and Justice belongs to Magic's illustrious cycle of “swords,” many of which command considerable sums on the secondary market. According to recent market prices, the original non-foil printing hovers around $29, while certain premium versions fetch even more.

The three-mana artifact gives its equipped creature +2/+2 and protection from both white and blue, already a formidable combination. However, its combat damage ability is where things get particularly nasty. Whenever the equipped creature damages an opponent, its controller places a +1/+1 counter on a creature they control, then proliferates, adding another counter of each existing kind to any number of eligible permanents or players.

For decks revolving around +1/+1 counters, proliferate, or even planeswalkers, that represents an abundance of value from a single successful attack. And since the Equipment costs just two mana to attach, it's easy to keep the sword swinging away.

The artwork is arguably just as appealing as the card's impressive abilities. Whereas 2024's Extra Life collection, Pixel Perfect, embraced the 16-bit era, this year's installment advances a console generation with chunky, low-polygon character models and distinctly late-'90s RPG aesthetics. The result looks like something that might have occupied a treasured memory card back in 1998.

Given how collectible Magic's more unusual Secret Lair treatments have become, this particular sword seems destined to attract attention.

Sword of Truth and Justice might be the proverbial final boss of this collection, but Balefire Dragon is hardly a pushover. Originally printed in 2011's Innistrad, this seven-mana, 6/6 flying Dragon has become a beloved Commander menace, with ordinary non-foil copies generally commanding between $15 and $20.

Its ability is delightfully straightforward: Whenever Balefire Dragon deals combat damage to a player, it deals that much damage to every creature that player controls. One successful attack from an unmodified Balefire Dragon can leave an opponent staring at six damage across their entire army. Ouch.

Together, Sword of Truth and Justice and Balefire Dragon represent roughly $45 or more in existing card value, depending on the printings being compared. That already exceeds the $39.99 asking price of the non-foil drop, before accounting for the remaining cards.

Those include Young Pyromancer, the popular two-mana "spells-matter" creature that produces Elemental tokens whenever its controller casts an instant or sorcery, and Big Score, a useful red instant that converts discarded cards into fresh draws and Treasure tokens. Neither is particularly expensive, but both are eminently playable.

It's another welcome round of reprints from Wizards, which recently announced the long-overdue return of the roughly $18 Soul Spike after two decades without another printing.

Rise of the Polygons | Extra Life 2026 goes on sale November 16 through Magic's official Secret Lair storefront, with the sale ending November 30 at 1 a.m. PT. Non-foil copies cost $39.99, while traditional foil versions retail for $49.99.

Collectors attending MagicCon: Atlanta from November 13–15 will also have an opportunity to purchase the collection early, although quantities will be limited.

The charitable component deserves attention, too. Half of the online sales price, minus third-party transaction fees, will support Extra Life and Seattle Children's Hospital. At MagicCon, Wizards says all proceeds from its Extra Life merchandise and the Rise of the Polygons drop will go toward the cause.

It's an admirable initiative with some genuinely desirable reprints, especially for Commander enthusiasts. And for anyone who spent their childhood battling suspiciously angular dragons with impossibly oversized swords, the artwork is a rather charming bonus.

At least this time, beating the dragon won't require blowing into a cartridge.

10-Acre Florida Ranch With NBA-Level Facility Is Listed for $38.9 Million in Celebrity Enclave Where Gisele Bündchen Lives

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

An enormous 9.69-acre Florida compound with a brand-new 10,500-square-foot entertainment facility "that can be transformed into anything you can imagine" and resort-inspired grounds has just hit the market in Southwest Ranches for an astonishing $38.9 million.

If it reels in anywhere near its asking price, the 23,442-square-foot estate could become one of the top sales in the ultrahigh net worth neighborhood that celebrities, professional athletes, and supermodel Gisele Bündchen call home.

"I have never seen a property done to this scale before," says Tommy Crivello of the Tommy Crivello Real Estate Group, who is co-listing the property with Angeline Earnest of Coldwell Banker Realty.

"It has something for the entire family as well as the privacy to be independent. The way it is designed now, it is big enough for an end user who loves it and wants to use it as a primary residence, but it could also be someone who wants to come down six months out of the year."

Once the home of Mexican singer Juan Gabriel, the luxury residence last traded hands for $9,000,000 in 2022. Its asking price of $1,659 per square foot is currently the highest-priced listing in the exclusive Sunshine Ranches enclave of Southwest Ranches.

"Everything has been completely redone with extensive remodeling," Earnest notes. "The landscaping, garage, pools, and the 10,000-square-foot recreational facility are all new. Every structure also has a new roof."

The sprawling property boasts a five-bedroom primary residence, a three-bedroom guesthouse with a catering-grade kitchen, a members-style clubhouse with a full bar and private theater, and a gym and spa wellness retreat with a massage room and cold plunge.

Private lakes, immersive art, koi ponds, "producing orchards," a Tiki pavilion, and two pools, including one with a custom 10-foot rock-jumping wall, can also be found on the enormous lot. An outdoor basketball court and hockey rink round out the property.

"There are 10 acres of walkable space," she adds. "It feels like the whole property is livable and is not overwhelming."

Crivello called Southwest Ranches the "Hamptons of South Florida" for its "rural-luxe" privacy, but it's also ideally located just minutes away from private aviation in Miami and Fort Lauderdale.

"We are seeing a lot of high-end celebrities coming here," Crivello says. "I see an entrepreneur, hedge fund person, athlete, or real big car collector buying this property. It could also be someone who is looking for a place for the entire family, as it is designed for multigenerational living."

A custom-engineered lighting system transforms the pathways into nocturnal works of art. For equestrians, the sale also includes plans for a seven-stall barn with oversized paddocks.

"The property is large enough that there is room for another outdoor facility, and it has direct access to miles of riding trails," he says. "Everything in Southwest Ranches has rural zoning, so you can have a garden and any kind of animal on property. It just flows effortlessly."

Created as a "fully integrated private campus," the serene property also features art installations and curated gathering spaces for entertaining.

“You can get to the beach in 30 minutes, Amerant Bank Arena in 15 minutes, Las Olas Boulevard for the nightlife, and Pembroke Gardens is just 10 minutes away,” Crivello says. “You can retreat to the country, or you can be in the limelight as much as you want to be.”

Built in 1964, the Chicago brick-clad main estate boasts a renovated interior with two guest suites, each with a private entrance and that “function as private apartments within the residence.”

A lavish upstairs primary wing has a breakfast bar, a refrigerator, a seating area, dual bathrooms, separate closets, and a private terrace.

Other highlights include an executive office, a chef’s kitchen, a built-in wine preservation system, a custom bar, and a wraparound covered balcony on the main level.

A detached garage pavilion spanning nearly 2,000 square feet is fully air-conditioned. The four-vehicle structure has lift capability to accommodate up to eight vehicles. It also has floor drains, integrated compressor and vacuum systems, EV-ready outlets, workstation walls, and extra storage space.

One of the most impressive features of the property is its enormous entertainment facility, “currently home to a nonprofit basketball program.”

The “NBA-level” structure features professional athletic flooring, locker rooms, conference spaces, a viewing balcony, and a smoothie bar.

Its flexible design could also be reimagined into a 50- to 100-vehicle auto showroom, a private museum, a production studio, or an event venue.

“The nice thing about the facility is that it has a second mezzanine where you can have spectators watch,” Crivello notes.

“You could also transform it into an indoor equestrian facility. It offers the peace of mind that upscale riders want. There has been a big shift for people who are car collectors, so the facility could also be transformed into an auto showroom. The building is huge and would be a focal point for an equestrian, athlete, or car collector.”

United Airlines Launches 11 New International Routes This Winter: See Full List Now

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

United Airlines is the second-largest international operator from the US, behind American Airlines. Of course, United's strength is long-haul, for which it is comfortably number one. In fact, the Star Alliance member is the world's second-largest long-haul carrier, after Emirates.

On Sunday, October 25, United (like other Northern Hemisphere airlines) switched to winter schedules based on IATA slot seasons. This will be maintained until Saturday, March 27, 2027. In this period, Cirium Diio data shows that United will have an average of 350 daily international departures, up by 2% compared to last winter. 11 additional routes help.

Schedule analysis shows that United will introduce, or resume, the following routes beyond the US border during the cold season. They are from five hubs. As they have all been announced, this article simply summarizes them and provides some context and information.

The October 31 launch of United's first service from Newark to St. Croix is not included. That is because the Caribbean destination is in the US Virgin Islands and is, therefore, considered domestic. United's three flights from Las Vegas to Tokyo Narita in January 2027 for the Consumer Electronics Show are also not featured.

Washington Dulles to Los Cabos (above photo is of the relaunch)

Houston Intercontinental to Tuxtla Gutiérrez (the following photo is from the relaunch)

Daily 737 MAX 9 (only until January 4, 2027)

Houston Intercontinental to Santo Domingo

3 weekly 737 MAX 8 (only until January 3, 2027)

No, it's a brand-new market for United; it'll coexist with Japan Airlines

* October 25, 2026, to March 27, 2027, only

When all airlines and US airports are considered, Cirium shows that United is the second-largest operator from the US to Mexico, behind American. United serves the transborder country from eight hubs and other airports. On a typical day, it has 86 daily departures to Mexico. For very obvious reasons, most of them — 46 daily and 53% of the total — are from Houston George Bush Intercontinental Airport (IAH).

After a 13-year absence, flights from IAH to Tuxtla Gutiérrez have resumed. As such, United now serves 25 destinations in Mexico from the Texas hub. They include Tulum, to which various US airlines have stopped flying. United will join them, as it is pulling out in January 2027. Service to Chihuahua returns at the start of the summer 2027 season.

16 of the 25 airports see the regional jets of United Express. Ten markets are exclusively in the hands of such equipment, whether on the E175 operated by Mesa or SkyWest, the CRJ550 with GoJet, or the ERJ145 with CommutAir. The 50-seat CRJ550 is on one route: Monterrey (from February 11, 2027). The ERJ145 also has only one market: Puebla, 669 nautical miles (1,237 km) away. Six routes see both RJs and non-RJ narrowbodies, while nine do not see RJs at all.

Only one of the transcontinental services have seen United before. Find out more here!

Starting on December 11, flights from San Francisco International Airport (SFO) to Sapporo will, of course, be a proper winter service. That's because the Japanese city is famous for being the gateway to skiing and other sporting activities and broader winter fun.

The other two upcoming long-haul routes — Los Angeles to Osaka and SFO to Okinawa — almost did not make the list. That's because the first departure from California will be on March 27, which is the final day of the northern aviation winter based on IATA slot seasons. The first service from Asia will be on March 28, the first day of the northern summer season. Okinawa will be a seasonal route, while Osaka will be flown year-round.

Terry Dubrow Reveals Cadaveric Fat Transfers Are a "Hot New" Procedure

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Terry Dubrow revealed a shocking truth, stunning Jenny McCarthy Wahlberg during his appearance on her podcast when he revealed fat transfers from cadavers are a new cosmetic trend.

Warning: You won’t be able to unlearn the knowledge Terry Dubrow recently dropped.

And, well, his intel about how plastic surgery and cadavers are connected cuts pretty deep.

But if you’re still reading this, fine. We’ll allow the Botched star himself to explain.

“The new thing is cadaveric fat transfer,” Dubrow informed Jenny McCarthy Wahlberg on the Nov. 3 episode of her eponymous podcast. “Have you heard of this?”

In response, an aghast McCarthy Wahlberg asked, “Does that mean from cadavers?”

“Yes,” Dubrow affirmed, sparking the Two and a Half Men alum to make a disgusted noise, before he added, “That’s the hot new thing.”

For the particularly macabre, here’s how it works. AlloClae, the name brand for such an injectable, is a non-surgical body contouring solution made from donated human fat tissue (yes, we’re talking about cadavers). The injectable is comprised of non-living fat cells, extracellular matrix, triglycerides and connective tissues.

The way alloClae is processed, per its website, preserves the natural 3D structure of fat cells, allowing for immediate volume without the downtime and invasiveness that comes with surgery.

For those whose minds are still reeling with concerns, Dubrow told McCarthy Wahlberg, “It's been processed and radiated and stuff like that. But there's different kinds of cadaver fat. One of the kinds, there's no cells in it. It's just the matrix and you injected it and your own fat grows into it.”

The 68-year-old emphasized, “That's the hot thing right now.”

Of course, while the trend might be new in the world of plastic surgery, the use of cadavers has been a regular practice in dentistry. Allograft bone is processed, sterilized bone harvested from deceased donors and is used in procedures such as dental bone grating, socket and ridge preservation as well as periodontal defect repair.

Though cadaveric fat transfer was a new one for McCarthy Wahlberg, allograft bone is a concept she’s previously sunk her teeth into—literally.

“Do you know how many dead bodies are in my mouth from all of my mouth surgeries that I've had?” the 54-year-old said to Dubrow. “Like, I've eaten more cadavers in the last two years than I ever thought I would.”

Nevertheless, she added, “I don't think I want cadaver's fat.”

But while cadaveric fat transfers are relatively fresh out the box, so to speak, plastic surgery itself has long existed within Hollywood. Read on for what some of your favorite celebrities have said about their cosmetic procedures—and how much they paid for them.

The Talk alum has never shied away from discussing her plastic surgeries."I’ve had a string of cosmetic procedures beginning with a browlift and facelift," she told The Telegraph in December 2023. "I had liposuction on my neck and had it lifted, too. I had my breasts lifted, my arms lipo-ed and my tummy tucked."Additionally, "I had my bum lifted and implants inserted," Osbourne continued. "Then I had a facelift three-and-a-half years ago. I was left with one eye higher than the other. It took a year to put it all right."As for how much this all cost?"Well, I haven’t kept a running total," Mrs. O admitted, "but it must top more than a million."

The Jennifer's Body actress has also been open about the changes she's made to her body, including a nose job, boob jobs, Botox and filler. And while she hasn't revealed the price tag for all her procedures, she has given a dollar-amount for her breast augmentations."My mom said that I used to go into the bathroom, and stand on the toilet, and pull up my shirt to see if my boobies had grown, and I would get really upset that I didn't have big boobs," Fox said on Alex Cooper's Call Her Daddy podcast in March 2025. "Thirty thousand dollars later, now I do."

Want to know right now (na na na) what the singer paid for the hair transplant he got in Turkey?"For what I did, it would've cost me $50,000 in America," he said on The Bootleg Kev Podcast in October 2022. "I paid, like, $7,800-$7,500."But then, Akon ended up shaving his head."I did the transplant to grow hair. The problem was my hair wasn't going," he noted on the podcast in April 2025. "So, I said, 'F--k it. I'm just going to keep it cut to keep it even.' But I actually like it better this way."

The comedian is taking those laughs all the way from the bank, revealing the hefty cost of her third facelift."It was not cheap," she noted on Josh Peck and Ben Soffer's Good Guys podcast in November 2025. "You ready? $218,000."

The price of the Dance Moms alum's veneers could be considered near the top of the pyramid."Most expensive thing I'm wearing? My teeth," she told BuzzFeed at the GLAAD Media Awards in March 2024. "These motherf--kers cost me $50,000."

Because The Real Housewives of Salt Lake City star is all about the receipts, proof, timeline and screenshots, she's given a detailed account of the plastic surgery and cosmetic work she's had done.And while Gay gets a lot of it for free as the owner of a medical spa, she's still racked up a grand total."Counting all of the things I’ve had done, I’m probably $200,000 into my physical appearance," she told Bustle in November 2025 about her list, including Botox, filler, two nose jobs, blepharoplasty and $30,000 veneers, "and it was worth every penny."

Confessing the price did give her pause, the talk show host's view on her lower deep plane facelift has been nothing but positive."Why do I look like this? $90,000," she told People in July 2026. "I was worried about the cost. I don’t like to waste money. And I would never spend that on a car."

The Soup alum is ladling up an estimate for a hair transplant—one of the many he's been open about undergoing."Ten thousand dollars," he said on Mark Normand and Sam Morril's We Might Be Drunk podcast in July 2025. "It might've gone up."And McHale would know as he shared he's gotten the procedure more than once for maintenance. "I'm fully bald," he stated. "This is four transplants."

In ‘Elsinore,’ Billie Piper Found “Beautiful Chaos” With Andrew Scott — And a New Career Chapter

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The decorated stage and screen star is a scene-stealer in the weepy Oscar contender, leading to the most attention she's received for a film role to date. The experience is inspiring her to forge a new path.

At the SCAD Savannah Film Festival, just ahead of Halloween weekend, Billie Piper held a masterclass for college students. “It was basically me just rattling on about being an actor — I hope some of it was useful, not sure,” she says the next day from a quiet library in the Southern city’s downtown. “Their line of questioning was so insightful and kind of ahead of their years. They were all there with the little moleskin books taking notes. I just didn’t expect that.”

She’d have little reason to. Despite her decades in the business, despite the Olivier Award win and five BAFTA nominations, the English native hasn’t really traveled the film-festival circuit before. She came to town as an honoree for the Oscar contender Elsinore, receiving the Marquee Award for her vivacious turn as the imperious real-life casting director Patsy Pollock. Related Stories Movies Golden Globes: 'Club Kid' Submitted as Drama (Exclusive) Movies Santa Barbara Film Fest: Inde Navarrette, Jaafar Jackson Among Eight Tapped for Virtuoso Awards

In spending time with the kids before the festivities kicked off, it dawned on her: “I actually thought: ‘God, I’ve done some really great jobs.’”

She arrives today with dark brown hair, a far cry from the striking blonde of Elsinore and her other most noted screen roles, and a tablet that she occasionally flashes open to reference movies she’s either seen recently or intends to soon watch. “It’s really fired me up about independent movies again,” she says of running around in support of Elsinore. (The film launched at Telluride, where star Andrew Scott emerged as an instant best-actor front-runner, and has since played all over the world.) “I felt like it was all dead, but it wasn’t dead. I just feel so jacked about all the movies I’m seeing.”

An intensely chameleonic actress unafraid to go big, Piper has never had the largest footprint in the feature space. “Often the films that I read weren’t that good for women,” she says. We may be at something of a turning point. Already, she’s received her first career British Independent Film Award nomination for Elsinore, competing in a supporting category alongside returning faces like Claire Foy and Tom Courtenay.

After breaking out as a teen pop star, the now-44-year-old has always followed the best work. She made her name onscreen by leading series like Doctor Who and Secret Diary of a Callgirl, then as a major stage actor in West End productions of The Effect and Yerma. For the latter revival of Federico García Lorca’s classic, she remains the only actor to have won every official Best Actress prize currently awarded for a single UK theater performance. She wowed again in HBO Max’s I Hate Suzie, a brilliant panic attack of a dark comedy about a moderately famous actress thrown into turmoil when nude photos on her phone leak online.

For one reason or another, these projects didn’t land with as much force in the US as back home. Indeed, Piper co-created I Hate Suzie with her Effect playwright Lucy Prebble, and the show aired right after other stellar series from British female multihyphenates, like Phoebe Waller-Bridge’s Fleabag and Michaela Coel’s I May Destroy You. Rest assured, Piper’s BAFTA-nominated performance is every bit as ferociously compelling — and revealing — as what her peers delivered in those Emmy-winning smashes.

For a good chunk of American audiences, at least, Suzie remains a hidden streaming gem waiting to be discovered. Piper seems comfortable in that zone.

Elsinore dramatizes the heartbreaking, inspiring final act of Ian Charleson, a gay actor not out publicly who was known for his starring role in Chariots of Fire. Charleson decided, shortly after being diagnosed with AIDS, to play Hamlet at the National Theatre while fighting for his life — and somehow pulled it off. Scott’s turn as the late great dominates the movie, but Piper becomes its sneaky heart. Patsy, Charleson’s best friend, emerges as his caretaker and as an anchor in their defiant chosen family.

“Their relationship was beautiful chaos,” Piper says. “They partied hard. He was a big figure in her daughter’s life. She was a single mother working in a male-dominated industry.” When it came to Charleson’s controversial choice to play Hamlet — his doctor, portrayed by Olivia Colman, was initially firmly against it — Patsy simply got it. So did Piper. “It makes complete sense to want to play one of, if not the most challenging parts at your most vulnerable point in your life as you approach your death,” Piper says. “Patsy understood that about him. I understand that about what he chose to do as well. I think a lot of actors get it, to be honest.”

Elsinore is a tearjerker, no doubt, but it’s stuffed with joy. Piper brings plenty of levity with her glamorous, haughty embodiment of an old showbusiness powerhouse. She is clearly having a ball. “I really miss people like Patsy in our industry — the larger-than-life characters — because everything’s so serious and there’s no money now,” Piper says. “It was fun to pull from the characters I knew in my early career. There were agents I had who were cut from a different cloth. It wasn’t all terribly kind, but it was unlike anybody I’d grown up with — the flamboyance, the drama, the quick shifts in tone and mood.”

Her chemistry with Scott is undeniable. The two veterans of the London theater scene previously worked together on Lena Dunham’s critically acclaimed Catherine Called Birdy, including a birthing scene for the ages. Like Piper, Scott is plenty decorated back home, but his frequent co-star finds him unusually playful to work with.

“With that skillset, you expect someone who’s a bit of a dick, but he’s not remotely a dick — he is warm, he is generous, he is informal. The work feels casual,” Piper says of Scott. “He takes it seriously, of course — he knows what he wants and knows what he likes — but it’s not the only thing that’s happening in his life, which I think is healthy.”

Piper can relate. She’s a mother of three and values the separation of the personal and professional. “I do my work, then I go home and am a parent — and so there’s no real processing of what I’ve done in the day, which is often really thrilling and rare and something I’d wanted to do since I was a child,” she says. Take, for instance, the job she’s just come back from: Wednesday season three. Piper grew up on Tim Burton and was now surrounded by his and his team’s singular designs. She got to act opposite the likes of Winona Ryder. And she was gone for nine months, a very long time. Too long, maybe.

“I haven’t worked on a nine-month shoot since Doctor Who, and I left that show for a reason,” she says. “The gratitude is there, but also it’s not without a cost.” Now, of course, she’s even further away, in the American south: “I’m doing a lot of being away. I feel terrible and also excited.”

Some viewers read into the whirring, control-obsessed anxiety of Piper’s I Hate Suzie tour-de-force as a thinly veiled commentary on her own struggles as an artist in the public eye. (Suzie’s career beginnings mirrored Piper’s to some degree.) The show was fiction, of course, but Piper’s guttural performance came from somewhere true.

“It’s really fun to play,” Piper says now, “but it’s just no way to live your life.”

The lesson took some learning. “I need things to be beyond good for me to take it seriously, to take myself seriously — it’s really got nothing to do with anyone else, but with insane levels of achievement,” Piper says. “It’s dysfunctional. I’ve been like that since I was a kid.” She believed the work should feel painful, that it should take everything out of her. She credits Yerma, which she performed in London and New York from 2016-2018 under the direction of Simon Stone — who so happens to be the director of Elsinore — with ushering in a new way forward in that regard.

“Simon completely changed my career for me — completely changed the way I looked at acting,” Piper says. “That’s a nice thing to have in a career that has been going on for a long time, to suddenly work with someone that completely rips up the rule book.”

Brick by brick, Piper is building a thrilling new chapter on that experience. Elsinore has proven a cozy fit. Not just as an awards-bound prestige movie offering her a meaty supporting role — also in the way it’s welcoming her into spaces she’s never even thought much of before. Her main goal this awards season? To hang out with Club Kid writer-director-star Jordan Firstman. “I’ve watched Rotting in the Sun about five times. It’s so funny and so free,” she says, referring to Sebastián Silva’s graphic meta comedy that starred Firstman as a vapid version of himself. “Now I just want to be his best mate.”

She’s written a film that she describes as “romantic horror,” which she’s planning to direct. (Her feature debut, Rare Beasts, premiered at Venice in 2019.) She knows it will be difficult to get off the ground. But she’s been feeling inspired by what she’s seeing. “It’s really useful going into a process of trying to get a film made that may never happen, may never see the light of day — it’s energized my passion,” she says.

Same goes for the students with whom she spent the previous afternoon.

“Even though we live in this difficult day and age, even though it might feel more compromised and frightening in terms of a career choice, they still want to pursue this,” Piper says. “There is still this appetite for the arts. I find it quite moving.”

Elsinore opens in limited North American theatrical release on November 20.

JetBlue is raising checked bag fees again — to as much as $70 for a first bag

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

The airline cited higher fuel and operating costs; the new fees apply to tickets booked on or after Nov. 3

JetBlue Airways raised its checked bag fees on Tuesday, with a first bag costing as much as $70 on peak travel dates when the fee is paid less than a day before the flight leaves, according to CNBC.

For a non-peak period, a first bag in economy costs $50 if paid for more than a day ahead of the flight and $60 if paid inside that 24-hour window, according to CNBC. During peak periods such as the holidays and summer, the fee is $55 in advance and $70 within 24 hours, up from $49 for the advance fee.

The new rates apply to any ticket purchased starting Nov. 3, according to Thrifty Traveler. Customers who booked earlier pay the old rates. A second bag costs $70 off-peak and $75 at peak when paid in advance, and a third bag costs $200 regardless of travel dates.

The first bag fee climbed from $60 to $80 each way for travelers buying the lowest-priced basic economy ticket on a transatlantic trip to Europe, according to Thrifty Traveler.

Customers who carry JetBlue's co-branded credit card or have elite frequent flyer status are exempt from the fees, according to CNBC. JetBlue Mint and BlueFirst passengers also receive a minimum of one complimentary checked bag, according to Thrifty Traveler.

"We're adjusting checked bag fees to help offset rising fuel and operating costs," JetBlue said in a statement, according to CNBC. "This helps us keep our base fares competitive while giving customers the choice to pay for a checked bag only if they need one."

The cost of jet fuel has approximately doubled this year, ever since the war in Iran began in February. Jet fuel was priced at $4.44 per gallon in the U.S. Gulf of Mexico market on Tuesday.

JetBlue was the first major U.S. airline to increase baggage charges following the outbreak of the war in late March, according to Thrifty Traveler. Every U.S. carrier matched that increase within weeks.

As much as $4.3 billion in direct visitor spending is expected to benefit hotels, restaurants, and retailers, according to a study from the Los Angeles County Economic Development Corporation commissioned by LA28.

Airbnb is looking for a bigger slice of that spending.

Attendance for the week ending November 1 was 251,700, down 4% from the previous week and 9% year-to-year.

Sam Darnold’s cratering play is a big picture problem for the Seahawks

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Seattle Seahawks quarterback Sam Darnold is a Super Bowl champion who played an important part in bringing another Lombardi Trophy to Seattle, and that can never be taken away.

Sam Darnold is also playing bad football.

You can blame the offensive coordinator, you can blame the offensive line, you can blame the lack of a running game, but Sam Darnold is playing bottom-tier football. His worst throw from Monday night's blowout loss against the Chicago Bears didn't even count, but it's emblematic of his incomprehensible decision-making.

Incidentally, the play design is pretty much DOA but there is no need to turn 2nd and 1 into a double coverage gamble.

Darnold's touchdown-to-interception ratio is barely 1:1 over his last 16 regular season starts. His mostly great playoff run is the break-up in what is otherwise a prolonged stretch of play that is far removed from much of his 2024 Minnesota Vikings season and the first 10-ish games of his Seahawks career.

(Editor's note: It's 344/551 and not 334)

There are no advanced stats that can positively spin Darnold as an above-average starter at the moment. He really wasn't playing particularly well for most of the back-end of 2025, but the running game finally sprung to life and Seattle's defense and special teams repeatedly either scored or gave the Seahawks extremely short fields.

The 2026 Seahawks are 4-4, which is not the end of the world, but how they've gotten to 4-4 is pretty much the worst case scenario in the short- and long-term. If Mike Macdonald's defense hypothetically had curiously dropped off to be a middling group and the Seahawks were losing shootouts while Darnold was putting up impressive numbers, I think we could live with that and acknowledge that defenses can be more volatile year-to-year than offenses. Darnold is heavily culpable for at least three of the four defeats, and even his best game against the Washington Commanders is significantly impacted by what was effectively a game-losing pick-six.

Darnold's contract is a great mid-level contract for someone with his track record and roughly two seasons worth of good productivity. It's also a contract that expires in 2027, and the current version of Darnold does not look like someone who should be Seattle's Week 1 starter, much less the starter in 2028.

Barring substantial improvement over the second half of the season, John Schneider has a major decision to make on Darnold, regardless of whether or not Brian Fleury is still the offensive coordinator. We've seen Darnold be very good when paired with the right play-caller, and I am not going to put the failures of this offense exclusively at his feet. But Darnold is in that tier of quarterback who needs way more help to be functional than the likes of Josh Allen or Joe Burrow or Patrick Mahomes. Every quarterback needs at least some semblance of foundational support to succeed, but Sam needs way more stuff than, say, prime Russell Wilson. That includes, seemingly above all else, having an experienced and effective play-caller who can play to his strengths and minimize his worst habits. Brian Fleury does not seem to fit the bill.

Darnold also needs play-action to work like we need air to breathe. When it's not working, Darnold has consistently been anywhere from average to ruinous in pure dropback situations. He needs an offensive line to protect him so well that his pressure rate is low, otherwise this is what you get.

Darnold with a clean pocket and/or the ability to get the ball out of his hands quickly can sling it with the best of them. When he is dealing with pressure in his face, he is barely a starting caliber quarterback. We have two years of evidence in a Seahawks uniform.

Play-action to be so insanely efficient that it overrides the lack of non-PA success.

Favorable game scripts such that his team is almost never trailing.

Related: A top-5 caliber defense such that his team is rarely trailing.

An offensive coordinator/play-caller who can scheme up easy-button throws for fun.

An offensive line that can pass protect competently.

An explosive running game in case none of the above works out or is figured out by NFL defenses.

There's nothing wrong with building a great environment around a competent but limited quarterback, but that also means a crevice turns into the Grand Canyon when said environment is even marginally worse. You can live with Darnold's turnovers if he's as efficient as he was in 2024 and 2025. That is not even close to what we're seeing in 2026.

The possibility also cannot be discounted that two years of film effectively exhausted all of the routes that the likes of Kevin O'Connell and Klint Kubiak could make Darnold thrive. As I wrote not too long ago, "With non-elite quarterbacks there are only so many levers you can pull before competent defenses respond." If Seattle sticks with Sam then it will likely need an(other) offensive coordinator to get him back on track, all while not being an immediate head coach candidate. This is the downside of having a defensive minded coach and your OC hires are either bad (see: Grubb, Ryan) or are so good that NFL teams take notice (see: Kubiak, Klint). The churn truly never stops unless you find that unicorn who can be a stable part of your organization while being no threat to hold a head coaching job.

Seattle's roster as a whole is a very good one; most of the players from last year's Super Bowl squad are still here. Any premise of Seattle having a sustained window of playoff and Super Bowl contention was contingent upon Darnold sustaining his success and mercifully giving us all a break from debating Seattle's next franchise quarterback. If Darnold cannot recapture even half of what made him go from draft bust to unlikely Super Bowl champion—this includes the rest of this year and (if applicable) his contract year in 2027—the Seahawks' long-term vision is suddenly a lot more complicated. Drew Lock is a free agent and frankly I don't want to bank on the QB revival thing yet again for a career backup entering Year 9. It's not like Drew himself hasn't had a history of being turnover prone with volatile week-to-week performances when he was a starter elsewhere. Jalen Milroe is a great unknown outside of preseason, and 2027 is a pivotal year for him to see if he's even ready to be an NFL starter. Other than that? Seattle might find itself once again dealing with major uncertainty at the most important position in the sport.

That's what stings the most, even coming off an unforgettable Super Bowl season. The Seahawks have their head coach, have a lot of hyper-talented, young core pieces under contract either on rookie deals or massive extensions, but quarterback has become a giant question mark now and therefore makes for a much murkier future than hoped.

PropStream’s Webinar Series ‘Connect to Close: Build Your 2027 Launch Plan’ Returns

What's New At Costco: November 2026 Edition

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

We may receive a commission on purchases made from links.

November has arrived, and while it's still fall and the time to enjoy the flavors of the season, retailers and consumers alike are gearing up for the holidays, beginning with Thanksgiving and continuing on to Christmas, Hanukkah, and other celebrations. As such, shelves at your favorite stores are being cleared of Halloween decorations and bags of trick-or-treat candy in favor of cornucopia motifs, pumpkin pies (some of which we've ranked), and turkeys. You may also begin to see Christmas trees, ornaments, and gift sets appear to inspire customers' holiday gift-giving.

Like every month, Costco has announced new items that it's carrying in stores and online. The popular warehouse club is known for offering great value on bulk items, including multipacks of many popular brands. It also has many exclusive items that you may be hard-pressed to find at other stores, and you can now find an ever-increasing array of international products, too, along with items from its in-house Kirkland Signature brand. Below are our picks for some of the most noteworthy items available in November. They include some holiday-ready essentials and beautiful gifts for loved ones and yourself, including a decadent dessert for Thanksgiving. You'll also find some everyday essentials. Just keep in mind that availability and prices may vary by location.

Toblerone Biscoff Milk Chocolate Truffles with Caramelized Biscuit Filling are available at Costco for $11.99.

Caramel apples are a favorite seasonal treat typically associated with fall, and the Mrs. Prindables brand has added a festive touch to these confections that makes them perfect for the winter holidays. Each one is covered in caramel and then dipped in a layer of chocolate, and you get a mix of triple chocolate and chocolate and walnut varieties in each pack. They're individually wrapped and include holiday stickers and decorative bows, making them perfect for gifting.

Mrs. Prindables Happy Holidays Caramel Apples are available at Costco in a six-pack for $59.99.

Wild boar was once something that you could only find at gourmet markets or through mail-order companies, but it's become more common at supermarkets, and you can now get a whole shoulder of it at Costco. Imagine pork but with a richer flavor and a hint of nuttiness, and you can understand why it's prized by many chefs. You can use this cut to make one impressive centerpiece for a holiday spread or divide it into smaller portions to use in different dishes, including a variety of styles of chili.

Northfork Wild Boar Boneless Shoulder is available at Costco in a 5-pound package for $119.99.

Olipop is one of Food Republic's top-ranking prebiotic sodas that also has less sugar than traditional options. This limited-time winter variety pack includes cola, ginger ale, and a new pomegranate flavor. They also all feature a cute yeti on the can, along with some seasonal graphics that remind you that the holidays are just around the corner.

Olipop Soda, Winter Variety Pack is available at Costco for $23.99.

This gift box is perfect for those who love Swedish-style gummies and features 12 2-ounce containers, each holding a different type of chewy treat.
